<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the rescaled counting measure of a critical square-lattice Fortuin–Kasteleyn Dobrushin interface in the unit square converges to the Minkowski-content measure of its Schramm–Loewner limit, for every fixed cluster weight $`1\le q\lt 4`$. A single deterministic constant times the predicted power of the mesh gives the normalization. Convergence is joint with the ordered curve and includes the total mass, giving the scaling limit of the interface's total number of steps.

- **claim-joint-natural-occupation-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For each fixed $q\in[1,4)$ there is a deterministic constant $c(q)\in(0,\infty)$ such that, along all positive integers $n$, $$\bigl([\eta_n],\nu_{n,c(q)}\bigr) \ \Longrightarrow\ \bigl([\eta],\mu_\eta\bigr).$$ The first coordinate has the ordered curve topology <a href="#eq:curve-metric" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:curve-metric">[eq:curve-metric]</a>, and the second has the weak topology of finite measures on $\overline D$. The two limiting coordinates belong to the same SLE sample. In particular, $$c(q)n^{-d}N_n\ \Longrightarrow\ \mu_\eta(\overline D)$$ jointly with the curve and all continuous spatial tests of its measure.
  - Explanation: The ordered interface and its rescaled traversal measure converge jointly to the same SLE sample and its Minkowski-content measure, including total mass.
- **claim-radial-regeneration-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-radial)): Fix $1\le q<4$, fixed radius-comparison constants, and a compact interval $V\subset(0,\infty)$. The following bounds are uniform over the above circuit and plane states and over $v\in V$.* *For states $\gamma,\gamma'$ of sizes $R,R'\ge r_0$, $$\label{eq:radial-comparison} \left\|\log\frac{F(\gamma;v)}{F(\gamma';v)}\right\| \le C\bigl(1+\|\log(R/R')\|\bigr).$$ There is $\delta>0$ such that every $F(\gamma;ve^w)$ is nonzero for $\|w\|<\delta$. Its logarithm, continued from its real value at $w=0$, has absolute value at most $C\log(2+R)$. Moreover $$\label{eq:radial-complex-ratio} \left\|\log\frac{F(\gamma;ve^w)}{F(\gamma;v)} -\log\frac{F(\gamma';ve^w)}{F(\gamma';v)}\right\| \le C\|w\|\bigl(1+\|\log(R/R')\|\bigr).$$* *For two states of comparable size $R$, their positively tilted laws can be coupled so that their configurations agree inside a common surrounding primal-open circuit containing $B(u,s)$, except with probability $C(s/R)^c$, for $r_0\le s\le R/C$. Conditional on the common circuit, the common continuation has its circuit law tilted by $v$.* *Finally, let $F_{\ge h}(\gamma;v)$ count only the eligible loops with $R_u(\ell)\ge h$. For comparable starts of size $R$, $$\label{eq:radial-truncation} \frac{F_{\ge\epsilon R}(\gamma;v)/ F_{\ge\epsilon R}(\gamma';v)} {F(\gamma;v)/F(\gamma';v)}=1+o(1)$$ uniformly as $\epsilon\downarrow0$ and $\epsilon R\to\infty$. The same real comparison and regeneration bounds hold with any such lower cutoff on the counted radii.
  - Explanation: Radial partition functions have controlled scale ratios, zero-free complex neighborhoods, inward couplings, and cancellation of sufficiently small counted loops.
- **claim-normalized-multipoint-correlations** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-normalization)): Suppose $p$ is fixed, $\max_j\|u_j\|\le Cn$, and $a=\min_{i\ne j}\|u_i-u_j\|\ge r_0$. Around every parameter whose singleton weights $v_{\{j\}}$ are strictly positive real numbers, the normalized correlation $$\label{eq:normalized-correlations} \mathcal C_n(\mathbf v;\mathbf u) =\frac{C(\mathbf v;\mathbf u)} {\prod_{j=1}^p F_n(v_{\{j\}})}$$ is holomorphic and bounded in a fixed complex neighborhood of the singleton weights, uniformly when the other weights range over a bounded set, with bound $C(n/a)^C$. For $a\ge cn$, if $u_j/n$ converge to distinct points and the point parities are fixed, <a href="#eq:normalized-correlations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:normalized-correlations">[eq:normalized-correlations]</a> has a limit locally uniformly in these complex parameter neighborhoods.* *When all nontrivial weights lie in a fixed compact subset of $(0,\infty)$ and $a\ge cn$, the normalized correlations are bounded above and below by positive constants. Their tilted laws are local at each point in the following quantitative sense. Consider two possibly different collections with bounded point counts, all weights in the stated positive compact set, and sizes comparable to the same $n$, satisfying the same fixed geometric bounds. Align one chosen point in each by a lattice symmetry, transporting its color, and suppose its singleton weight is the same in both. The two laws admit a coupling with identical configuration inside a common surrounding circuit containing $B(u,s)$, with error $C(s/n)^c$. For minimum separation $a$ the analogous error is at most $C(n/a)^C(s/a)^c$, whenever $r_0\le s\le a/C$.
  - Explanation: Dividing multipoint correlations by local nesting masses gives holomorphic bounds and macroscopic limits. Positive tilts also admit quantitative local couplings.
- **claim-macroscopic-tests-with-insertions** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-macroscopic-test)): Let $u_j/n$ converge to distinct points, with their point parities fixed, and let $P_j$ be disjoint round patches centered at $u_j$, of radii fixed positive multiples of $n$, with disjoint larger collars. Fix $v_j>0$ and define $$U_n=\prod_jv_j^{\#\{\ell:u_j\text{ inside }\ell,\ \ell\subset P_j\}}.$$ Suppose $T_n$ is a uniformly bounded discretization, $\sup_n\\|T_n\\|_\infty<\infty$, of a fixed bounded functional $T$ of the rescaled plane loop collection, determined in a fixed bounded region by loops of diameter at least a fixed $\delta>0$. Suppose that this functional is almost surely continuous for the plane loop limit, with its marked-point, patch-boundary and contact tests taken at continuity radii. Here discretization means that $T_n(\omega_n)\to T(\omega)$ whenever the relevant rescaled loop collections $\omega_n$ converge to a continuity configuration $\omega$. Then the following sequence converges as $n\to\infty$: $$\label{eq:radial-macroscopic-test} \frac{\mathbb E[U_nT_n]}{\prod_jF_n(v_j)}.$$* *The conclusion also applies to tests obtained by first excluding arbitrarily large excursions, provided the discarded part tends to zero in probability under the normalized $U_n$ law uniformly in $n$. Boundedness of $T_n$ may be replaced by $\|T_n\|\le K^{X_n}$, where $X_n$ is the sum of a fixed finite family of macroscopic passage counts satisfying $$\label{eq:radial-test-integrability} \sup_n\frac{\mathbb E[U_ne^{tX_n}]}{\mathbb EU_n}<\infty \qquad\text{for every fixed }t<\infty,$$ also after the inner-radius truncations of the local weights. The bounded truncations of the test must satisfy the preceding continuity requirement.
  - Explanation: Macroscopic loop tests converge after normalization by local insertion masses, subject to continuity and suitable uniform integrability.
- **claim-finite-current-spectrum** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-spectral)): Almost every spectral function on $\mathcal D$ is either $1$ or a finite Blaschke product with $$\label{eq:finite-spectral-inner} f(0)=f(\infty)=1, \qquad f(\overline w)=\overline{f(w)}.$$ There is a spectral vector $\mathsf h$, not necessarily of finite norm, such that $$\label{eq:current-cocycle} J(w)\Omega=(1-f(w))\mathsf h.$$ The measure $\mu$ given by the squared fiber norm of $\mathsf h$ is invariant under dilation. On the cross-section of products whose smallest zero has modulus one, it decomposes as $$\label{eq:current-disintegration} \mu=\,\mathrm ds\,\nu(\,\mathrm dF),\qquad f(w)=F(e^s w), \qquad \nu(1)<\infty.$$ If $\mathcal W(F)$ is the difference between the largest and smallest logarithmic zero moduli, and $2n_F$ is the degree, then, for some $\eta_0>0$, $$\label{eq:current-width-tail} \nu\{\mathcal W>t\}\le C e^{-2\eta_0t},\qquad \int n_F\,\nu(\,\mathrm dF)<\infty.$$ The physical plane height has logarithmic variance. More precisely, after an axial symmetry, write a nonzero displacement as $(k,j)$ with $k\ge1$ and $\|j\|\le k$. Then $$\label{eq:spectral-variogram} \begin{split} \mathbb E\bigl(h(k,j)-h(0,0)\bigr)^2 &=2\Re\int\bigl(1-f(1)^k f(\pm i)^{\|j\|}\bigr)\,\mu(\,\mathrm df)\\ &=4\nu(1)\log k+O(1), \end{split}$$ where the sign agrees with the direction of the second coordinate. The error is uniform over these displacements.
  - Explanation: The diagonal current spectrum consists of finite inner products and the constant function. A dilation-invariant current measure has width and degree integrability sufficient for the logarithmic height variogram.
- **claim-off-axis-laplacian-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-off-axis)): There are constants $c_*>0$ and $C<\infty$, depending on the fixed parameter and on $p,M$, such that, if $z$ has distance $r\ge10$ from the axis containing the insertions, then $$\label{eq:off-axis-laplacian} \|\Delta_2H(z)\|\le C n^{Cb}r^{-2-c_*}.$$ For each fixed experiment, unit increments in either coordinate direction tend to zero when their base points tend to infinity along either normal to the insertion axis.
  - Explanation: Vacuum spectral damping gives improved Laplacian decay away from the insertion axis and vanishing unit increments along its normals.
- **claim-twisted-coordinate-differences** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-twisted-differences)): Suppose a row interval contains no insertion, has one fixed unit twist, and leaves at least $l$ physical rows on each side of an observation stencil. For every fixed $k\ge1$, every $k$-fold coordinate difference with steps of length two satisfies $$\label{eq:twisted-derivative-bound} \|D_2^{(k)}H(z)\|\le C_k n^{Cb}l^{-k},\qquad l\ge10k.$$ The estimate is uniform in the twist and in the spatial position of the stencil. The directions of the differences may be mixed.
  - Explanation: Twisted row estimates control mixed coordinate differences near the insertion axis when the observation stencil has insertion-free rows on both sides.
- **claim-summable-potential-laplacian** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-laplacian)): For the neutral small-charge experiments of Equation <a href="#eq:electric-characteristic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:electric-characteristic">[eq:electric-characteristic]</a>, there are $c_1>0$ and $C<\infty$ such that $$\label{eq:laplacian} \|\Delta_2H(z)\|\le C n^{Cb} \left(1+\min_j\|z-u_j\|\right)^{-2-c_1} \qquad(z\in\mathbb Z^2).$$ The constants are uniform over the stated geometry and charges.
  - Explanation: Combining the off-axis estimate with twisted differences yields a summable Laplacian error measured from the nearest insertion.
- **claim-coulomb-correlation-amplitude** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-coulomb)): Fix a number $p$ of marked points. There are $b,c,\xi>0$ and a real-analytic function $f$ on a neighborhood of $[-b,b]$, with $f(0)=0$, such that, for neutral real charges $\max_j\|t_j\|\le b$ and distinct points on a tile axis satisfying $$\max_j\|u_j\|\le Mn,\qquad \min_{i\ne j}\|u_i-u_j\|\ge n^{1-\xi},$$ one has $$\label{eq:coulomb} \log C(\mathbf t;\mathbf u) =\sum_{j=1}^p f(t_j) +2A\sum_{i<j}t_it_j\log\|u_i-u_j\|+O(n^{-c}), \qquad f(0)=0.$$ Here the same local function $f$ is used for every number of points, on their common small-charge neighborhood; it is independent of the geometry and of the point parities. The constants can depend on $p,M$ and $q$.* *For either choice of sign, the corresponding multiplicative statement has analytic continuation along compact paths from zero in $$\mathcal D_p^\pm =\{\mathbf t\in\mathbb R^p:\ \sum_jt_j=0, \ \pm t_j\in I_\lambda\text{ for every }j\}.$$ More precisely, on a sufficiently thin complex neighborhood of each such compact path within the neutral hyperplane $\sum_jt_j=0$, there is a holomorphic function $M_p$ such that $$\label{eq:coulomb-continued} \frac{C(\mathbf t;\mathbf u)} {\displaystyle\prod_{i<j}\|u_i-u_j\|^{2At_it_j}} =M_p(\mathbf t)+O(n^{-c}).$$ The positive constants $c$ and $\xi$ may decrease with the path. Near zero, $M_p(\mathbf t)=\exp(\sum_jf(t_j))$. The error in <a href="#eq:coulomb-continued" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:coulomb-continued">[eq:coulomb-continued]</a> is additive; a relative error is asserted only where $M_p$ is nonzero.
  - Explanation: Separated neutral axial charges have a Coulomb interaction with a universal local contribution and a power remainder. Analytic continuation gives additive amplitude control, becoming relative where the amplitude is nonzero.
- **claim-individual-nesting-amplitudes** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-pure-nest)): There is $\varepsilon_0>0$ and a nonvanishing holomorphic function $a(t)$ near $[-\varepsilon_0,\varepsilon_0]$, positive for real $t$, such that $$F_n(v(t))=a(t)n^{\beta(t)}(1+o(1))$$ locally uniformly on a complex neighborhood of that interval. The limit uses the fixed cutoff and rounding in the definition of $F_n$. No rate is asserted for this individual amplitude.
  - Explanation: Individual positive nesting partition functions have a nonvanishing holomorphic amplitude near zero charge. The result does not supply an amplitude convergence rate.
- **claim-nonzero-special-core-amplitude** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-special-charges)): Two separately neutral groups of the preceding kind can be chosen so that their combined amplitude in <a href="#eq:coulomb-continued" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:coulomb-continued">[eq:coulomb-continued]</a> is nonzero. The formula then has a relative power error, including when the two $\alpha$-points have opposite colors and least separation $r\ge n^{1-\xi}$, for a sufficiently small fixed $\xi>0$.* *Place the first core at the origin, the second at odd axial separation $r\asymp n^{1-\xi}$, and all compensators at distinct positions $n z_j+O(1)$, with fixed nonzero axial $z_j$. Write $B_* =\sum_{j\,\mathrm{remote}}\beta(t_j)$. There is a constant $M_*\ne0$, depending only on those charges, their macroscopic positions, and the fixed microscopic conventions, such that $$\label{eq:special-close-pair} C_r(n)=M_*n^{B_*-x}r^{2A\alpha^2}(1+O(n^{-c})) =M_*n^{B_*}r^{-2x_1}(r/n)^x(1+O(n^{-c})), \qquad x_1=-\beta(\alpha).$$ The same conclusion is uniform for $n^{1-\xi}\le r\le n^{1-\xi'}$, with fixed $0<\xi'\le\xi$, after decreasing $c$. For $r=an$ with fixed small $a>0$, the full Coulomb product applies; its remaining geometric factor tends to $M_*$ as $a\downarrow0$ and stays bounded above and away from zero.
  - Explanation: Separately neutral core groups can be selected with nonzero combined amplitude. Their close-pair asymptotic connects the Coulomb interaction to the arm exponents.
- **claim-sharp-one-arm-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-one-arm)): There are constants $0<c<C<\infty$ such that $$\label{eq:one-arm} cR^{-x_1}\le\pi_1(R)\le CR^{-x_1}, \qquad x_1=-\beta(\alpha),$$ for all radii above the fixed microscopic scale.
  - Explanation: The monochromatic arm probability is bounded above and below by the same power determined by the core charge.
- **claim-logarithmic-two-arm-exponent** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-two-arm-log)): For every $\delta>0$ there is $C_\delta<\infty$ such that $$\label{eq:two-arm-log} C_\delta^{-1}(r/R)^{x+\delta} \le\pi_2(r,R)\le C_\delta(r/R)^{x-\delta}, \qquad R\ge r\ge r_0,$$ where $r_0$ is a fixed microscopic scale.
  - Explanation: Alternating annular arms satisfy upper and lower power bounds with arbitrarily small exponent slack above a fixed microscopic scale.
- **claim-three-start-probabilities** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-start-probabilities)): There is a fixed minimum radius $r_0$ such that, for $R$ sufficiently larger than the start, $$\label{eq:start-probabilities} \mathbb P(S_R)\asymp \begin{cases} \pi_2(r_0,R),&\text{for a tile-side start},\\ \pi_1(r)^2\pi_2(r,R),&\text{for two sites at separation }\asymp r,\\ \pi_2(r,R),&\text{for a disk of radius }r. \end{cases}$$ The same constants apply in an ambient FK law with a full bulk collar beyond the target cut, including after a nonnegative integrable reweighting supported beyond that collar. In the second line, the constants can depend on the fixed comparability bounds for the two sites.
  - Explanation: Tile-side, separated-site, and disk starts have probabilities comparable to their corresponding arm factors, uniformly under permitted nonnegative remote reweightings.
- **claim-outward-conditioned-coupling** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-outward)): Fix $q\in[1,4)$. Consider one ambient FK law whose graph is the full tile lattice throughout the region bounded by the target cut and in a fixed-ratio bulk collar beyond it. All imposed wires and nontrivial identifications are supported outside this region and collar. The law may be multiplied by a common nonnegative integrable function supported beyond the collar, with positive finite normalizing expectation. Let two starts of the types above be contained in a disk of radius $s$, where $1\le s\le R/C$. Their laws, conditioned on their respective events $S_R$, have a coupling such that, except with probability $$\label{eq:outward-error} C(s/R)^c,$$ the configurations agree on and outside an annular band lying between the starts and the target cut. There are exactly two strand subarcs joining the rims of this band. Each start reaches the same transmitted primal region and the same transmitted dual region of the band. Consequently the exterior sees the same pairing of its exposed strand ends in the two configurations.* *Here $c>0$ and $C<\infty$ depend only on $q$, the fixed collar geometry, and the fixed comparability constants of the starts. The assertion is uniform in the exterior reweighting.
  - Explanation: Conditioned starts can share a separating band and exterior with power accuracy. The band transmits the same color regions and exposed strand pairing.
- **claim-reference-separating-band** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-reference-band)): At every sufficiently large scale $l$, there is a fixed-shape square annular band $\mathcal B_l$, of inner and outer radii comparable to $l$, and an event of probability bounded below in the plane FK law with the following properties. Both colors cross $\mathcal B_l$; there are exactly two strand through-arcs; and each of the two crossing color regions has separated landing extensions beyond both rims. The extension boxes can be chosen a positive relative distance from the central band and from each other. The constants and the relative sizes of all these regions are independent of $l$.
  - Explanation: A positive-probability annular construction has exactly the strand through-arcs needed to transmit a unique region of each color, with separated extensions beyond both rims.
- **claim-signed-core-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-signed-comparison)): Consider two opposite-color core pairs contained in a disk of radius $s$, with the same remote charges and local reweighting. If $Cs<R<cn$, then $$\left\|\mathbb E_n^*[Y_1\mid S_R(1)]- \mathbb E_n^*[Y_2\mid S_R(2)]\right\| \le C(s/R)^{c_1}.$$ The constants are independent of the microscopic separations of the pairs.
  - Explanation: Outward coupling and moment bounds compare conditional signed factors for core pairs without requiring the factors themselves to be positive.
- **claim-neighboring-pair-pure-amplitude** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-microscopic-amplitude)): For the fixed remote experiment there exists $c_*\ne0$ such that $$\label{eq:microscopic-amplitude} C_e(n)=c_*n^{B_*-x}(1+O(n^{-c_3})).$$ In particular this limit holds through all positive integers $n$.
  - Explanation: The neighboring-pair correlation has a nonzero pure-power amplitude with a power error through the full integer sequence.
- **claim-sharp-alternating-arms** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-sharp-arms)): For all radii above a fixed lattice scale, $$\label{eq:sharp-arms} \mathbb P(S_R(e))\asymp R^{-x},\qquad \pi_2(r,R)\asymp(r/R)^x.$$ The estimates hold with fixed-factor changes of the inner and outer rims and with a further bulk collar.
  - Explanation: Microscopic reaching events and alternating annular arms have matching sharp power comparisons, stable under the stated rim changes and bulk collars.
- **claim-disk-diagnostic-limit** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-disk-diagnostic)): For almost every fixed pair of scaled radii $0<\epsilon<\theta$ in the permissible range, with $s=\epsilon n$ and $R=\theta n$, the limit $$\label{eq:diagnostic-limit} \lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-B_*}W_s(n)$$ exists. Tile-rim roundings do not change the limit.
  - Explanation: The normalized disk diagnostic has a limit at almost every permissible pair of scaled radii, and bounded tile-rim rounding does not change it.
- **claim-deterministic-disk-calibration** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-disk-calibration)): There is a sequence $\epsilon\downarrow0$, a choice of radii $R/n\asymp\epsilon^b$, and positive deterministic numbers $L_\epsilon\asymp\epsilon^{-x}$ such that $$\label{eq:disk-calibration} \limsup_{n\to\infty} \left\| \frac{n^x\mathbb P(S_R(e))}{L_\epsilon\mathbb P(S_R(\epsilon n))}-1 \right\|\le C\epsilon^{c_7}.$$ The exponent $b>0$ may be chosen sufficiently small in advance to satisfy any additional finitely many inequalities of the form $(1-b)c>bx$, with $c>0$ fixed. The same constants apply to every translate and rotation of the edge start, including color exchange.
  - Explanation: Along selected shrinking disk radii, positive deterministic factors calibrate disk reaching probabilities against microscopic edge probabilities with vanishing relative error.
- **claim-second-moment-probe-replacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-l2-replacement)): For every real $f\in C_c(D)$, $$\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathbb E\bigl[\|X_n(f)-X_{n,\epsilon}(f)\|^2\bigr]=0, \label{eq:l2-replacement}$$ where $\epsilon$ tends to zero through the sequence in Proposition <a href="#prop:disk-calibration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:disk-calibration">26</a>. The same approximation holds for every finite vector of such tests.
  - Explanation: Calibrated disk probes replace microscopic counting tests in second moment, first taking the mesh limit and then shrinking the admissible disk radii.
- **claim-whole-square-minkowski-content** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-whole-content)): Let $\eta$ be chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_\kappa$ in $(D;a,b)$, with the parameters of Equation <a href="#eq:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameters">[eq:parameters]</a>. Its interior $d$-dimensional Euclidean Minkowski-content measure extends by zero on $\partial D$ to a finite random measure $\mu_\eta$ on $\overline D$. For every real $f\in C(\overline D)$, $$r^{-x}\int_\mathbb Cf(\Pi z) \mathbf 1_{\{\operatorname{dist}(z,\operatorname{tr}\eta)<r\}}\,\mathrm dA(z) \longrightarrow \mu_\eta(f) \quad\hbox{in probability as }r\downarrow0. \label{eq:whole-content-convergence}$$ Its expected total mass is finite, and some nonnegative $f\in C_c(D)$ has $\mathbb E\mu_\eta(f)>0$. If $U_\delta=\{z\in\overline D:\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial D)\le\delta\}$, then $$\mathbb E\mu_\eta(U_\delta)\le C\delta^{1-x}. \label{eq:continuum-boundary-strip}$$
  - Explanation: Interior SLE content extends to a finite measure on the closed square with zero boundary mass. Projected whole-plane neighborhood approximations converge, and expected boundary-strip mass vanishes.
- **claim-standing-chordal-trace-convergence** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inp-trace)): For the marked uniform polygon approximations of a bounded marked Jordan domain used in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the critical Dobrushin interface converges in law to chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_\kappa$ in the uniform Euclidean oriented-curve topology modulo increasing reparametrization.
  - Explanation: The standing trace input supplies convergence of the oriented interface shape modulo increasing reparametrization. It does not supply occupation convergence.
- **claim-standing-fk-localization-estimates** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inp-fk)): The following estimates hold for critical square-lattice FK at the fixed parameter $q$. 1. An actual crossing of a discrete simple-polygon quadrilateral with bounded extremal distance has probability bounded below uniformly in the boundary partition. The dual statement holds as well. Arm separation, quasi-multiplicativity, and localization to fixed separated landing intervals hold for each fixed color word, in particular for one arm and for a primal–dual pair. 2. If two positive laws agree on a collar between radii $r$ and $16r$, their marginals beyond its fixed side have mutually bounded densities. Equivalently, expectations of every nonnegative function on the remote edge set are comparable. This holds for a full annulus, for a bounded number of sectors separated by straight coordinate walls with matching deterministic wire portions, and for a fixed-shape rectangular bulk collar. Coincident banks of a slit are treated as distinct boundary incidences. 3. Through $k$ sufficiently separated full annuli, the ratio of remote expectations improves to $1+O((1-c)^k)$, or both expectations are zero. The assertion holds in either radial direction and after positive modifications supported on the unobserved side. 4. If $X$ counts disjoint raw strand passages through a fixed buffered annulus or one of the preceding sectors, then, for every fixed $B<\infty$, $$\mathbb E\bigl[e^{BX}\mid\text{exterior}\bigr]\le C_B.$$ The bound is uniform in scale and in the exterior-induced boundary partition. It also holds for a fixed finite family of such counts. 5. Let $r_j$ be dyadic radii and $N_j$ the number of raw nests wholly inside $r_j$, in a geometry extending beyond the largest cutoff by a buffer. A bulk nest is a closed loop surrounding the marked point. The increment satisfies $0\le N_j-N_{j-1}\le X_j$, where the buffer of $X_j$ is disjoint from $B_{r_{j-k_0}}$ for a fixed $k_0$. For $b>0$, set $F_j=\mathbb Eb^{N_j}$ and $\,\mathrm d\nu_j=F_j^{-1}b^{N_j}\,\mathrm d\mathbb P$. Then $$C_b^{-1}\le\frac{F_j}{F_{j-1}}\le C_b, \qquad \mathbb E_{\nu_j}e^{BX_i}\le C_{b,B}\quad(i\ge j).$$ These statements remain valid conditionally on exterior data of the specified geometry, and with a fixed family of buffered passage counts adjoined at or beyond the outer tilt level. The constants can be chosen uniformly for $b$ in a compact subset of $(0,\infty)$.
  - Explanation: The standing FK input supplies crossing and arm estimates, comparison through specified collars, exponential passage moments, and positive local tilt bounds.
- **claim-standing-nested-plane-loop-limit** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inp-loops)): The entire loop collection of the free infinite-volume critical square-lattice FK law converges, through the full mesh sequence, to nested whole-plane $\mathrm{CLE}_\kappa$. Convergence is in spherical loop-matching topology, with curves compared modulo their traversal parametrizations, and includes all nested generations. The limit is invariant under similarities and, more generally, Möbius maps.
  - Explanation: The standing plane-loop input supplies convergence of the entire nested ensemble through the full mesh sequence, with loop matching and similarity and Möbius invariance of the limit.
- **claim-standing-physical-angular-representation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inp-rows)): At $0<\lambda\le\pi/3$, the normalized homogeneous rows have a plane Hilbert-space representation with vacuum $\Omega$. Finite ordinary spin and diagonal phase words have the expectations of the preceding physical plane law. With the angular coordinate normalized so that the strip is $\|\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z\|<\pi/2$, marked rows $A^D(z)$ are bounded and holomorphic, satisfy $\\|A^D(z)\\|\le2\\|D\\|$, and obey $$A^D(z+ib)=U_bA^D(z)U_b^{-1},\qquad U_b=e^{ibB},$$ where $B$ is self-adjoint. For a diagonal unitary twist, the normalized commuting row family has scalar spectral functions which, under $w=e^{iz}$, are inner in the right half-plane. They continue without zeros through a common-width band at each strip edge. In particular, their zeros lie in a fixed cone $\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}w_j\ge c_*\|w_j\|$, and their general factorization has the form $$f(w)=\eta\exp(-a_*w-b_*/w)\prod_j\beta_j(w),\qquad a_*,b_*\ge0,\quad \|\eta\|=1,$$ where $\beta_j$ are normalized half-plane Blaschke factors and $\sum_j\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}w_j/(1+\|w_j\|^2)<\infty$. The sum may initially be infinite.
  - Explanation: The standing angular-row input provides the physical plane representation, holomorphic row bounds, unitary covariance, and inner spectral factorization. The initial zero collection may be infinite.
- **claim-standing-separated-height-covariance** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inp-covariance)): For four distinct points $u,u',v,v'$ and lattice approximants tending to them, $$\begin{aligned} &\lim_{\delta\downarrow0} \mathbb E\bigl[(h_\delta(u')-h_\delta(u)) (h_\delta(v')-h_\delta(v))\bigr]\\ &\qquad=\sigma^2\bigl(G(u',v')-G(u',v)-G(u,v')+G(u,v)\bigr), \qquad \sigma^2=\frac{2}{\pi-\lambda}. \end{aligned}$$ The convergence is locally uniform away from endpoint collisions.
  - Explanation: The standing covariance input identifies the continuum covariance of separated height increments and its variance multiplier, locally uniformly away from endpoint collisions.

          "source_id": "PM-223.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $1\\le q<4$, fixed radius-comparison constants, and a compact interval $V\\subset(0,\\infty)$. The following bounds are uniform over the above circuit and plane states and over $v\\in V$.*\n\n\n\n*For states $\\gamma,\\gamma'$ of sizes $R,R'\\ge r_0$, $$\\label{eq:radial-comparison}\n \\left|\\log\\frac{F(\\gamma;v)}{F(\\gamma';v)}\\right|\n       \\le C\\bigl(1+|\\log(R/R')|\\bigr).$$ There is $\\delta>0$ such that every $F(\\gamma;ve^w)$ is nonzero for $|w|<\\delta$. Its logarithm, continued from its real value at $w=0$, has absolute value at most $C\\log(2+R)$. Moreover $$\\label{eq:radial-complex-ratio}\n \\left|\\log\\frac{F(\\gamma;ve^w)}{F(\\gamma;v)}\n       -\\log\\frac{F(\\gamma';ve^w)}{F(\\gamma';v)}\\right|\n \\le C|w|\\bigl(1+|\\log(R/R')|\\bigr).$$*\n\n*For two states of comparable size $R$, their positively tilted laws can be coupled so that their configurations agree inside a common surrounding primal-open circuit containing $B(u,s)$, except with probability $C(s/R)^c$, for $r_0\\le s\\le R/C$. Conditional on the common circuit, the common continuation has its circuit law tilted by $v$.*\n\n\n*Finally, let $F_{\\ge h}(\\gamma;v)$ count only the eligible loops with $R_u(\\ell)\\ge h$. For comparable starts of size $R$, $$\\label{eq:radial-truncation}\n \\frac{F_{\\ge\\epsilon R}(\\gamma;v)/\n             F_{\\ge\\epsilon R}(\\gamma';v)}\n      {F(\\gamma;v)/F(\\gamma';v)}=1+o(1)$$ uniformly as $\\epsilon\\downarrow0$ and $\\epsilon R\\to\\infty$. The same real comparison and regeneration bounds hold with any such lower cutoff on the counted radii.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $p$ is fixed, $\\max_j|u_j|\\le Cn$, and $a=\\min_{i\\ne j}|u_i-u_j|\\ge r_0$. Around every parameter whose singleton weights $v_{\\{j\\}}$ are strictly positive real numbers, the normalized correlation $$\\label{eq:normalized-correlations}\n \\mathcal C_n(\\mathbf v;\\mathbf u)\n   =\\frac{C(\\mathbf v;\\mathbf u)}\n          {\\prod_{j=1}^p F_n(v_{\\{j\\}})}$$ is holomorphic and bounded in a fixed complex neighborhood of the singleton weights, uniformly when the other weights range over a bounded set, with bound $C(n/a)^C$. For $a\\ge cn$, if $u_j/n$ converge to distinct points and the point parities are fixed, <a href=\"#eq:normalized-correlations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:normalized-correlations\">[eq:normalized-correlations]</a> has a limit locally uniformly in these complex parameter neighborhoods.*\n\n*When all nontrivial weights lie in a fixed compact subset of $(0,\\infty)$ and $a\\ge cn$, the normalized correlations are bounded above and below by positive constants. Their tilted laws are local at each point in the following quantitative sense. Consider two possibly different collections with bounded point counts, all weights in the stated positive compact set, and sizes comparable to the same $n$, satisfying the same fixed geometric bounds. Align one chosen point in each by a lattice symmetry, transporting its color, and suppose its singleton weight is the same in both. The two laws admit a coupling with identical configuration inside a common surrounding circuit containing $B(u,s)$, with error $C(s/n)^c$. For minimum separation $a$ the analogous error is at most $C(n/a)^C(s/a)^c$, whenever $r_0\\le s\\le a/C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-macroscopic-test",
        "exact_quote": "Let $u_j/n$ converge to distinct points, with their point parities fixed, and let $P_j$ be disjoint round patches centered at $u_j$, of radii fixed positive multiples of $n$, with disjoint larger collars. Fix $v_j>0$ and define $$U_n=\\prod_jv_j^{\\#\\{\\ell:u_j\\text{ inside }\\ell,\\ \\ell\\subset P_j\\}}.$$ Suppose $T_n$ is a uniformly bounded discretization, $\\sup_n\\|T_n\\|_\\infty<\\infty$, of a fixed bounded functional $T$ of the rescaled plane loop collection, determined in a fixed bounded region by loops of diameter at least a fixed $\\delta>0$. Suppose that this functional is almost surely continuous for the plane loop limit, with its marked-point, patch-boundary and contact tests taken at continuity radii. Here discretization means that $T_n(\\omega_n)\\to T(\\omega)$ whenever the relevant rescaled loop collections $\\omega_n$ converge to a continuity configuration $\\omega$. Then the following sequence converges as $n\\to\\infty$: $$\\label{eq:radial-macroscopic-test}\n \\frac{\\mathbb E[U_nT_n]}{\\prod_jF_n(v_j)}.$$*\n\n\n*The conclusion also applies to tests obtained by first excluding arbitrarily large excursions, provided the discarded part tends to zero in probability under the normalized $U_n$ law uniformly in $n$. Boundedness of $T_n$ may be replaced by $|T_n|\\le K^{X_n}$, where $X_n$ is the sum of a fixed finite family of macroscopic passage counts satisfying $$\\label{eq:radial-test-integrability}\n \\sup_n\\frac{\\mathbb E[U_ne^{tX_n}]}{\\mathbb EU_n}<\\infty\n \\qquad\\text{for every fixed }t<\\infty,$$ also after the inner-radius truncations of the local weights. The bounded truncations of the test must satisfy the preceding continuity requirement.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Here discretization means that $T_n(\\omega_n)\\to T(\\omega)$ whenever the relevant rescaled loop collections $\\omega_n$ converge to a continuity configuration $\\omega$. Then the following sequence converges as $n\\to\\infty$: $$\\label{eq:radial-macroscopic-test}\n \\frac{\\mathbb E[U_nT_n]}{\\prod_jF_n(v_j)}.$$*\n\n\n*The conclusion also applies to tests obtained by first excluding arbitrarily large excursions, provided the discarded part tends to zero in probability under the normalized $U_n$ law uniformly in $n$. Boundedness of $T_n$ may be replaced by $|T_n|\\le K^{X_n}$, where $X_n$ is the sum of a fixed finite family of macroscopic passage counts satisfying $$\\label{eq:radial-test-integrability}\n \\sup_n\\frac{\\mathbb E[U_ne^{tX_n}]}{\\mathbb EU_n}<\\infty\n \\qquad\\text{for every fixed }t<\\infty,$$ also after the inner-radius truncations of the local weights.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-spectral",
        "exact_quote": "Almost every spectral function on $\\mathcal D$ is either $1$ or a finite Blaschke product with $$\\label{eq:finite-spectral-inner}\n f(0)=f(\\infty)=1,\n \\qquad f(\\overline w)=\\overline{f(w)}.$$ There is a spectral vector $\\mathsf h$, not necessarily of finite norm, such that $$\\label{eq:current-cocycle}\n J(w)\\Omega=(1-f(w))\\mathsf h.$$ The measure $\\mu$ given by the squared fiber norm of $\\mathsf h$ is invariant under dilation. On the cross-section of products whose smallest zero has modulus one, it decomposes as $$\\label{eq:current-disintegration}\n \\mu=\\,\\mathrm ds\\,\\nu(\\,\\mathrm dF),\\qquad f(w)=F(e^s w),\n \\qquad \\nu(1)<\\infty.$$ If $\\mathcal W(F)$ is the difference between the largest and smallest logarithmic zero moduli, and $2n_F$ is the degree, then, for some $\\eta_0>0$, $$\\label{eq:current-width-tail}\n \\nu\\{\\mathcal W>t\\}\\le C e^{-2\\eta_0t},\\qquad\n \\int n_F\\,\\nu(\\,\\mathrm dF)<\\infty.$$ The physical plane height has logarithmic variance. More precisely, after an axial symmetry, write a nonzero displacement as $(k,j)$ with $k\\ge1$ and $|j|\\le k$. Then $$\\label{eq:spectral-variogram}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathbb E\\bigl(h(k,j)-h(0,0)\\bigr)^2\n &=2\\Re\\int\\bigl(1-f(1)^k f(\\pm i)^{|j|}\\bigr)\\,\\mu(\\,\\mathrm df)\\\\\n &=4\\nu(1)\\log k+O(1),\n \\end{split}$$ where the sign agrees with the direction of the second coordinate. The error is uniform over these displacements.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-spectral",

On the cross-section of products whose smallest zero has modulus one, it decomposes as $$\\label{eq:current-disintegration}\n \\mu=\\,\\mathrm ds\\,\\nu(\\,\\mathrm dF),\\qquad f(w)=F(e^s w),\n \\qquad \\nu(1)<\\infty.$$ If $\\mathcal W(F)$ is the difference between the largest and smallest logarithmic zero moduli, and $2n_F$ is the degree, then, for some $\\eta_0>0$, $$\\label{eq:current-width-tail}\n \\nu\\{\\mathcal W>t\\}\\le C e^{-2\\eta_0t},\\qquad\n \\int n_F\\,\\nu(\\,\\mathrm dF)<\\infty.$$ The physical plane height has logarithmic variance. More precisely, after an axial symmetry, write a nonzero displacement as $(k,j)$ with $k\\ge1$ and $|j|\\le k$. The error is uniform over these displacements.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-off-axis",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $c_*>0$ and $C<\\infty$, depending on the fixed parameter and on $p,M$, such that, if $z$ has distance $r\\ge10$ from the axis containing the insertions, then $$\\label{eq:off-axis-laplacian}\n |\\Delta_2H(z)|\\le C n^{Cb}r^{-2-c_*}.$$ For each fixed experiment, unit increments in either coordinate direction tend to zero when their base points tend to infinity along either normal to the insertion axis.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.5"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-twisted-differences",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a row interval contains no insertion, has one fixed unit twist, and leaves at least $l$ physical rows on each side of an observation stencil. For every fixed $k\\ge1$, every $k$-fold coordinate difference with steps of length two satisfies $$\\label{eq:twisted-derivative-bound}\n |D_2^{(k)}H(z)|\\le C_k n^{Cb}l^{-k},\\qquad l\\ge10k.$$ The estimate is uniform in the twist and in the spatial position of the stencil. The directions of the differences may be mixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-twisted-differences",

      "exact_statement": "Suppose a row interval contains no insertion, has one fixed unit twist, and leaves at least $l$ physical rows on each side of an observation stencil. For every fixed $k\\ge1$, every $k$-fold coordinate difference with steps of length two satisfies $$\\label{eq:twisted-derivative-bound}\n |D_2^{(k)}H(z)|\\le C_k n^{Cb}l^{-k},\\qquad l\\ge10k.$$ The estimate is uniform in the twist and in the spatial position of the stencil. The directions of the differences may be mixed.",
      "explanation": "Twisted row estimates control mixed coordinate differences near the insertion axis when the observation stencil has insertion-free rows on both sides.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-laplacian",
        "exact_quote": "For the neutral small-charge experiments of Equation <a href=\"#eq:electric-characteristic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:electric-characteristic\">[eq:electric-characteristic]</a>, there are $c_1>0$ and $C<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{eq:laplacian}\n |\\Delta_2H(z)|\\le C n^{Cb}\n       \\left(1+\\min_j|z-u_j|\\right)^{-2-c_1}\n \\qquad(z\\in\\mathbb Z^2).$$ The constants are uniform over the stated geometry and charges.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-coulomb",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a number $p$ of marked points. There are $b,c,\\xi>0$ and a real-analytic function $f$ on a neighborhood of $[-b,b]$, with $f(0)=0$, such that, for neutral real charges $\\max_j|t_j|\\le b$ and distinct points on a tile axis satisfying $$\\max_j|u_j|\\le Mn,\\qquad\n \\min_{i\\ne j}|u_i-u_j|\\ge n^{1-\\xi},$$ one has $$\\label{eq:coulomb}\n \\log C(\\mathbf t;\\mathbf u)\n =\\sum_{j=1}^p f(t_j)\n   +2A\\sum_{i<j}t_it_j\\log|u_i-u_j|+O(n^{-c}),\n \\qquad f(0)=0.$$ Here the same local function $f$ is used for every number of points, on their common small-charge neighborhood; it is independent of the geometry and of the point parities. The constants can depend on $p,M$ and $q$.*\n\n\n*For either choice of sign, the corresponding multiplicative statement has analytic continuation along compact paths from zero in $$\\mathcal D_p^\\pm\n =\\{\\mathbf t\\in\\mathbb R^p:\\ \\sum_jt_j=0,\n                         \\ \\pm t_j\\in I_\\lambda\\text{ for every }j\\}.$$ More precisely, on a sufficiently thin complex neighborhood of each such compact path within the neutral hyperplane $\\sum_jt_j=0$, there is a holomorphic function $M_p$ such that $$\\label{eq:coulomb-continued}\n \\frac{C(\\mathbf t;\\mathbf u)}\n {\\displaystyle\\prod_{i<j}|u_i-u_j|^{2At_it_j}}\n =M_p(\\mathbf t)+O(n^{-c}).$$ The positive constants $c$ and $\\xi$ may decrease with the path. Near zero, $M_p(\\mathbf t)=\\exp(\\sum_jf(t_j))$. The error in <a href=\"#eq:coulomb-continued\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:coulomb-continued\">[eq:coulomb-continued]</a> is additive; a relative error is asserted only where $M_p$ is nonzero.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-coulomb",

The constants can depend on $p,M$ and $q$.*\n\n\n*For either choice of sign, the corresponding multiplicative statement has analytic continuation along compact paths from zero in $$\\mathcal D_p^\\pm\n =\\{\\mathbf t\\in\\mathbb R^p:\\ \\sum_jt_j=0,\n                         \\ \\pm t_j\\in I_\\lambda\\text{ for every }j\\}.$$ More precisely, on a sufficiently thin complex neighborhood of each such compact path within the neutral hyperplane $\\sum_jt_j=0$, there is a holomorphic function $M_p$ such that $$\\label{eq:coulomb-continued}\n \\frac{C(\\mathbf t;\\mathbf u)}\n {\\displaystyle\\prod_{i<j}|u_i-u_j|^{2At_it_j}}\n =M_p(\\mathbf t)+O(n^{-c}).$$ The positive constants $c$ and $\\xi$ may decrease with the path.           "source_id": "PM-223.5"

      "exact_statement": "Fix a number $p$ of marked points.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-special-charges",
        "exact_quote": "Two separately neutral groups of the preceding kind can be chosen so that their combined amplitude in <a href=\"#eq:coulomb-continued\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:coulomb-continued\">[eq:coulomb-continued]</a> is nonzero. The formula then has a relative power error, including when the two $\\alpha$-points have opposite colors and least separation $r\\ge n^{1-\\xi}$, for a sufficiently small fixed $\\xi>0$.*\n\n\n*Place the first core at the origin, the second at odd axial separation $r\\asymp n^{1-\\xi}$, and all compensators at distinct positions $n z_j+O(1)$, with fixed nonzero axial $z_j$. Write $B_* =\\sum_{j\\,\\mathrm{remote}}\\beta(t_j)$. There is a constant $M_*\\ne0$, depending only on those charges, their macroscopic positions, and the fixed microscopic conventions, such that $$\\label{eq:special-close-pair}\n C_r(n)=M_*n^{B_*-x}r^{2A\\alpha^2}(1+O(n^{-c}))\n       =M_*n^{B_*}r^{-2x_1}(r/n)^x(1+O(n^{-c})),\n \\qquad x_1=-\\beta(\\alpha).$$ The same conclusion is uniform for $n^{1-\\xi}\\le r\\le n^{1-\\xi'}$, with fixed $0<\\xi'\\le\\xi$, after decreasing $c$. For $r=an$ with fixed small $a>0$, the full Coulomb product applies; its remaining geometric factor tends to $M_*$ as $a\\downarrow0$ and stays bounded above and away from zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-special-charges",

Write $B_* =\\sum_{j\\,\\mathrm{remote}}\\beta(t_j)$. There is a constant $M_*\\ne0$, depending only on those charges, their macroscopic positions, and the fixed microscopic conventions, such that $$\\label{eq:special-close-pair}\n C_r(n)=M_*n^{B_*-x}r^{2A\\alpha^2}(1+O(n^{-c}))\n       =M_*n^{B_*}r^{-2x_1}(r/n)^x(1+O(n^{-c})),\n \\qquad x_1=-\\beta(\\alpha).$$ The same conclusion is uniform for $n^{1-\\xi}\\le r\\le n^{1-\\xi'}$, with fixed $0<\\xi'\\le\\xi$, after decreasing $c$. For $r=an$ with fixed small $a>0$, the full Coulomb product applies; its remaining geometric factor tends to $M_*$ as $a\\downarrow0$ and stays bounded above and away from zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-223.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Two separately neutral groups of the preceding kind can be chosen so that their combined amplitude in <a href=\"#eq:coulomb-continued\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:coulomb-continued\">[eq:coulomb-continued]</a> is nonzero. For $r=an$ with fixed small $a>0$, the full Coulomb product applies; its remaining geometric factor tends to $M_*$ as $a\\downarrow0$ and stays bounded above and away from zero.",
      "explanation": "Separately neutral core groups can be selected with nonzero combined amplitude. Their close-pair asymptotic connects the Coulomb interaction to the arm exponents.",

      "conclusion": {

        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed minimum radius $r_0$ such that, for $R$ sufficiently larger than the start, $$\\label{eq:start-probabilities}\n \\mathbb P(S_R)\\asymp\n \\begin{cases}\n   \\pi_2(r_0,R),&\\text{for a tile-side start},\\\\\n   \\pi_1(r)^2\\pi_2(r,R),&\\text{for two sites at separation }\\asymp r,\\\\\n   \\pi_2(r,R),&\\text{for a disk of radius }r.\n \\end{cases}$$ The same constants apply in an ambient FK law with a full bulk collar beyond the target cut, including after a nonnegative integrable reweighting supported beyond that collar.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-outward",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $q\\in[1,4)$. Consider one ambient FK law whose graph is the full tile lattice throughout the region bounded by the target cut and in a fixed-ratio bulk collar beyond it. All imposed wires and nontrivial identifications are supported outside this region and collar. The law may be multiplied by a common nonnegative integrable function supported beyond the collar, with positive finite normalizing expectation. Let two starts of the types above be contained in a disk of radius $s$, where $1\\le s\\le R/C$. Their laws, conditioned on their respective events $S_R$, have a coupling such that, except with probability $$\\label{eq:outward-error}\n C(s/R)^c,$$ the configurations agree on and outside an annular band lying between the starts and the target cut. There are exactly two strand subarcs joining the rims of this band. Each start reaches the same transmitted primal region and the same transmitted dual region of the band. Consequently the exterior sees the same pairing of its exposed strand ends in the two configurations.*\n\n*Here $c>0$ and $C<\\infty$ depend only on $q$, the fixed collar geometry, and the fixed comparability constants of the starts. The assertion is uniform in the exterior reweighting.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

There are exactly two strand subarcs joining the rims of this band. Each start reaches the same transmitted primal region and the same transmitted dual region of the band.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-reference-band",
        "exact_quote": "At every sufficiently large scale $l$, there is a fixed-shape square annular band $\\mathcal B_l$, of inner and outer radii comparable to $l$, and an event of probability bounded below in the plane FK law with the following properties. Both colors cross $\\mathcal B_l$; there are exactly two strand through-arcs; and each of the two crossing color regions has separated landing extensions beyond both rims. The extension boxes can be chosen a positive relative distance from the central band and from each other. The constants and the relative sizes of all these regions are independent of $l$.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.5"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-whole-content",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\eta$ be chordal $\\mathrm{SLE}_\\kappa$ in $(D;a,b)$, with the parameters of Equation <a href=\"#eq:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:parameters\">[eq:parameters]</a>. Its interior $d$-dimensional Euclidean Minkowski-content measure extends by zero on $\\partial D$ to a finite random measure $\\mu_\\eta$ on $\\overline D$. For every real $f\\in C(\\overline D)$, $$r^{-x}\\int_\\mathbb Cf(\\Pi z)\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\operatorname{dist}(z,\\operatorname{tr}\\eta)<r\\}}\\,\\mathrm dA(z)\n \\longrightarrow \\mu_\\eta(f)\n \\quad\\hbox{in probability as }r\\downarrow0.\n \\label{eq:whole-content-convergence}$$ Its expected total mass is finite, and some nonnegative $f\\in C_c(D)$ has $\\mathbb E\\mu_\\eta(f)>0$. If $U_\\delta=\\{z\\in\\overline D:\\operatorname{dist}(z,\\partial D)\\le\\delta\\}$, then $$\\mathbb E\\mu_\\eta(U_\\delta)\\le C\\delta^{1-x}.\n \\label{eq:continuum-boundary-strip}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-223.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-whole-content",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\eta$ be chordal $\\mathrm{SLE}_\\kappa$ in $(D;a,b)$, with the parameters of Equation <a href=\"#eq:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:parameters\">[eq:parameters]</a>. Its interior $d$-dimensional Euclidean Minkowski-content measure extends by zero on $\\partial D$ to a finite random measure $\\mu_\\eta$ on $\\overline D$. For every real $f\\in C(\\overline D)$, $$r^{-x}\\int_\\mathbb Cf(\\Pi z)\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\operatorname{dist}(z,\\operatorname{tr}\\eta)<r\\}}\\,\\mathrm dA(z)\n \\longrightarrow \\mu_\\eta(f)\n \\quad\\hbox{in probability as }r\\downarrow0.\n \\label{eq:whole-content-convergence}$$ Its expected total mass is finite, and some nonnegative $f\\in C_c(D)$ has $\\mathbb E\\mu_\\eta(f)>0$. If $U_\\delta=\\{z\\in\\overline D:\\operatorname{dist}(z,\\partial D)\\le\\delta\\}$, then $$\\mathbb E\\mu_\\eta(U_\\delta)\\le C\\delta^{1-x}.\n \\label{eq:continuum-boundary-strip}$$",

      "exact_statement": "Let $\\eta$ be chordal $\\mathrm{SLE}_\\kappa$ in $(D;a,b)$, with the parameters of Equation <a href=\"#eq:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:parameters\">[eq:parameters]</a>. Its interior $d$-dimensional Euclidean Minkowski-content measure extends by zero on $\\partial D$ to a finite random measure $\\mu_\\eta$ on $\\overline D$. For every real $f\\in C(\\overline D)$, $$r^{-x}\\int_\\mathbb Cf(\\Pi z)\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\operatorname{dist}(z,\\operatorname{tr}\\eta)<r\\}}\\,\\mathrm dA(z)\n \\longrightarrow \\mu_\\eta(f)\n \\quad\\hbox{in probability as }r\\downarrow0.\n \\label{eq:whole-content-convergence}$$ Its expected total mass is finite, and some nonnegative $f\\in C_c(D)$ has $\\mathbb E\\mu_\\eta(f)>0$. If $U_\\delta=\\{z\\in\\overline D:\\operatorname{dist}(z,\\partial D)\\le\\delta\\}$, then $$\\mathbb E\\mu_\\eta(U_\\delta)\\le C\\delta^{1-x}.\n \\label{eq:continuum-boundary-strip}$$",

        "exact_quote": "The following estimates hold for critical square-lattice FK at the fixed parameter $q$.\n\n1.  An actual crossing of a discrete simple-polygon quadrilateral with bounded extremal distance has probability bounded below uniformly in the boundary partition. The dual statement holds as well. Arm separation, quasi-multiplicativity, and localization to fixed separated landing intervals hold for each fixed color word, in particular for one arm and for a primal–dual pair.\n\n2.  If two positive laws agree on a collar between radii $r$ and $16r$, their marginals beyond its fixed side have mutually bounded densities. Equivalently, expectations of every nonnegative function on the remote edge set are comparable. This holds for a full annulus, for a bounded number of sectors separated by straight coordinate walls with matching deterministic wire portions, and for a fixed-shape rectangular bulk collar. Coincident banks of a slit are treated as distinct boundary incidences.\n\n3.  Through $k$ sufficiently separated full annuli, the ratio of remote expectations improves to $1+O((1-c)^k)$, or both expectations are zero. The assertion holds in either radial direction and after positive modifications supported on the unobserved side.\n\n4.  If $X$ counts disjoint raw strand passages through a fixed buffered annulus or one of the preceding sectors, then, for every fixed $B<\\infty$, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[e^{BX}\\mid\\text{exterior}\\bigr]\\le C_B.$$ The bound is uniform in scale and in the exterior-induced boundary partition. It also holds for a fixed finite family of such counts.\n\n5.  Let $r_j$ be dyadic radii and $N_j$ the number of raw nests wholly inside $r_j$, in a geometry extending beyond the largest cutoff by a buffer. A bulk nest is a closed loop surrounding the marked point. The increment satisfies $0\\le N_j-N_{j-1}\\le X_j$, where the buffer of $X_j$ is disjoint from $B_{r_{j-k_0}}$ for a fixed $k_0$. For $b>0$, set $F_j=\\mathbb Eb^{N_j}$ and $\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_j=F_j^{-1}b^{N_j}\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbb P$. Then $$C_b^{-1}\\le\\frac{F_j}{F_{j-1}}\\le C_b,\n     \\qquad \\mathbb E_{\\nu_j}e^{BX_i}\\le C_{b,B}\\quad(i\\ge j).$$ These statements remain valid conditionally on exterior data of the specified geometry, and with a fixed family of buffered passage counts adjoined at or beyond the outer tilt level. The constants can be chosen uniformly for $b$ in a compact subset of $(0,\\infty)$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Critical FK and SLE parameters** ([source](#eq:parameters)): Fix $q\in[1,4)$ and put $$\label{eq:parameters} p_q=\frac{\sqrt q}{1+\sqrt q},\qquad \kappa=\frac{4\pi}{\arccos(-\sqrt q/2)},\qquad d=1+\frac{\kappa}{8},\qquad x=2-d.$$
- **Traversal counting measure** ([source](#eq:counting-measure)): For $c>0$, set $$\label{eq:counting-measure} \nu_{n,c}=c n^{-d}\sum_{j=1}^{N_n}\delta_{\Pi(z_{n,j})}.$$
- **Ordered curve distance** ([source](#eq:curve-metric)): For continuous oriented curves $\gamma_1,\gamma_2:[0,1]\to\overline D$, use the distance $$\label{eq:curve-metric} d_{\mathrm{curv}}(\gamma_1,\gamma_2) =\inf_{\phi_1,\phi_2}\sup_{0\le t\le1} \|\gamma_1(\phi_1(t))-\gamma_2(\phi_2(t))\|,$$ where the infimum is over increasing homeomorphisms of $[0,1]$.
- **Projected whole-trace Minkowski content** ([source](#eq:content-measure)): Its $d$-dimensional Minkowski-content measure is normalized by $$\label{eq:content-measure} \mu_{\eta,r} =r^{-x}\Pi_*\bigl[ \mathbf 1\{\operatorname{dist}(z,\operatorname{tr}\eta)<r\}\,\mathrm dA(z)\bigr], \qquad \mu_{\eta,r}\longrightarrow\mu_\eta \quad\text{in probability as }r\downarrow0.$$
- **Multipoint loop correlation** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Assign relative weights $\mathbf v=(v_I)$, with $v_\varnothing=v_{\{1,\ldots,p\}}=1$, and define $$C(\mathbf v;\mathbf u) =\mathbb E\prod_{\varnothing\ne I\subsetneq\{1,\ldots,p\}} v_I^{N_I}.$$
- **Reference nesting mass** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Let $b_0\in(1,2)$ be a fixed continuity cutoff for the limiting loop law, and write $$F_n(v)=\mathbb Ev^{\#\{\ell:u\text{ inside }\ell, R_u(\ell)\le b_0n\}}.$$
- **Neutral electric characteristic function** ([source](#eq:electric-characteristic)): For real charges $t=(t_1,\ldots,t_p)$ with $\sum_jt_j=0$, define $$\label{eq:electric-characteristic} U_t=\exp\left(i\sum_jt_jh(u_j)\right),\qquad C(t;u)=\mathbb EU_t.$$
- **Coordinate step-two Laplacian** ([source](#pm-h-18)): For a function on tile vertices, write $$\Delta_2H(z)=H(z+2e_1)+H(z-2e_1) +H(z+2e_2)+H(z-2e_2)-4H(z).$$
- **Microscopic occupation test** ([source](#eq:microscopic-test)): Define $$X_n(f)=A_0n^{-2}\sum_{e\in\mathcal E_n}f(z_e)n^x I_e =A_0n^{-d}\sum_{e\in\mathcal E_n}f(z_e)I_e. \label{eq:microscopic-test}$$

          "anchor": "eq:nest-log-exponent",
          "exact_quote": "The complex estimates of Section <a href=\"#sec:radial\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:radial\">3</a> turn this local calculation into a formula at finite charges. First they determine the scaling exponent of each positive nesting weight, without yet determining its amplitude. On thin complex neighborhoods of compact subintervals of $I_\\lambda$, the functions $$\\frac{\\log F_n(v(t))}{\\log n}$$ form a locally bounded holomorphic family; logarithms are continued from real positive weights. Extract any convergent subsequence. For small neutral systems, the all-positive comparison in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:normalization\">8</a> bounds the normalized correlation above and below. Equation <a href=\"#eq:coulomb\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:coulomb\">[eq:coulomb]</a>, with separations of order $n$, therefore shows that its limiting exponent $g$ satisfies $$\\sum_j g(t_j)=-A\\sum_jt_j^2.$$ The two- and three-charge identities imply $g(t)=-At^2+Bt$ near zero. Holomorphic uniqueness extends this identity along $I_\\lambda$. Since $$v(t)=v(-2\\lambda-t),$$ the same symmetry holds for $g$, and it forces $B=-2\\lambda A$. Every subsequential exponent is thus the same. We have proved, locally uniformly also in those complex neighborhoods, $$\\label{eq:nest-log-exponent}\n F_n(v(t))=\\exp\\{\\beta(t)\\log n+o(\\log n)\\},\n \\qquad \\beta(t)=-A(t^2+2\\lambda t).$$",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-outward",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $q\\in[1,4)$. Consider one ambient FK law whose graph is the full tile lattice throughout the region bounded by the target cut and in a fixed-ratio bulk collar beyond it. All imposed wires and nontrivial identifications are supported outside this region and collar. The law may be multiplied by a common nonnegative integrable function supported beyond the collar, with positive finite normalizing expectation. Let two starts of the types above be contained in a disk of radius $s$, where $1\\le s\\le R/C$. Their laws, conditioned on their respective events $S_R$, have a coupling such that, except with probability $$\\label{eq:outward-error}\n C(s/R)^c,$$ the configurations agree on and outside an annular band lying between the starts and the target cut. There are exactly two strand subarcs joining the rims of this band. Each start reaches the same transmitted primal region and the same transmitted dual region of the band. Consequently the exterior sees the same pairing of its exposed strand ends in the two configurations.*\n\n*Here $c>0$ and $C<\\infty$ depend only on $q$, the fixed collar geometry, and the fixed comparability constants of the starts. The assertion is uniform in the exterior reweighting.",
          "source_id": "PM-223.5"
        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-radial-regeneration** [claim](#stmt-prop-radial) / [proof](#pm-h-11): Control passage moments at circuit walls, build stopping circuits, couple asynchronous radial levels, and inductively continue partition-function logarithms. -> Radial scale comparisons, inward coupling, complex nonvanishing, and cutoff cancellation.
- **step-normalized-multipoint-limits** [claim](#stmt-prop-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-normalization): Introduce disjoint positive patch weights, bound macroscopic witnesses, cancel small-loop masses, and use holomorphic uniqueness. -> Normalized multipoint correlation limits and local tilted-law couplings.
- **step-macroscopic-test-extension** [claim](#stmt-cor-macroscopic-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-macroscopic-test): Screen local insertion cores from tested macroscopic loops and remove count and excursion truncations using uniform integrability. -> Convergence for normalized macroscopic tests with local insertions.
- **step-current-spectral-analysis** [claim](#stmt-prop-spectral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-spectral): Use marked row exchange to obtain dilation decay, exclude infinite inner factors, derive the current cocycle, and apply a local energy sum rule. -> Finite current spectrum, integrable orbit measure, and logarithmic height variance.
- **step-off-axis-spectral-damping** [claim](#stmt-lem-off-axis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-off-axis): Estimate the Laplacian spectral multiplier on pure and mixed zero bands, then sum the damped dyadic bands. -> Improved Laplacian decay off the insertion axis and vanishing normal increments.
- **step-twisted-near-axis-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-twisted-differences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-twisted-differences): Glue compressed twisted currents across the imaginary rays and combine low-band cancellation with damping from insertion-free rows. -> Uniform mixed coordinate finite-difference bounds.
- **step-global-summable-laplacian** [claim](#stmt-prop-laplacian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-laplacian): Move near-axis stencils normally to the region of spectral damping and control the accumulated change by higher finite differences. -> Summable Laplacian error away from the insertion points.
- **step-coulomb-profile-reconstruction** [claim](#stmt-prop-coulomb) / [proof](#pm-h-21): Construct local charge profiles, invert the Laplacian on parity cosets, integrate the charge derivative, and continue the resulting amplitude analytically. -> Coulomb interaction with a power remainder and covariance-calibrated stiffness.
- **step-pure-nesting-amplitude** [claim](#stmt-prop-pure-nest) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pure-nest): Compare neutral pair and triple amplitudes and remove the remaining linear ambiguity using reflection of the nesting weight. -> Nonvanishing individual nesting amplitude near zero charge.
- **step-special-nonzero-charges** [claim](#stmt-prop-special-charges) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-special-charges): Use the opposite winding convention and positive crossing constructions to prevent vanishing, then continue separately neutral groups. -> Nonzero special core amplitudes and the mesoscopic close-pair formula.
- **step-one-arm-calibration** [claim](#stmt-lem-one-arm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-arm): Identify the zero singleton loop weight with a color connection, extend the arm through buffered corridors, and cancel compensator powers. -> Sharp monochromatic one-arm comparison.
- **step-logarithmic-two-arm-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-arm-log) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-arm-log): Compare annular arms with Dobrushin disk hits, apply ordered-trace convergence and the SLE Green estimate, and iterate quasimultiplicativity. -> Alternating arm exponent bounds with arbitrarily small slack.
- **step-start-probability-factorization** [claim](#stmt-lem-start-probabilities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-start-probabilities): Separate local arm events by collars and glue their landings through disjoint color corridors. -> Probability comparisons for segment, separated-site, and disk starts.
- **step-reference-band-topology** [claim](#stmt-lem-reference-band) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reference-band): Construct primal and dual roads with flanking crossings and join them into a winding barrier with controlled color transitions. -> A positive-probability band with exactly the required through-arcs and separated protrusions.
- **step-conditioned-outward-coupling** [claim](#stmt-prop-outward) / [proof](#pm-h-28): Retain cluster-connected landing extensions, prove frequent good trials under arm conditioning, minorize central-band laws, and couple their common exterior continuation. -> Power-accuracy agreement of conditioned starts on a separating band and its exterior.
- **step-signed-factor-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-signed-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-signed-comparison): Match signed factors on successful outward coupling and bound their discrepancy on failure by Hölder and conditional moments. -> Absolute power comparison of conditional signed expectations.
- **step-microscopic-amplitude-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-microscopic-amplitude) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-microscopic-amplitude): Use a nonzero mesoscopic expectation to make signed comparison relative, compare fixed core radii across nearby scales, and sum the amplitude-ratio errors. -> Nonzero neighboring-pair amplitude through all integer scales.
- **step-sharp-two-arm-upgrade** [claim](#stmt-prop-sharp-arms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sharp-arms): Bound the microscopic reaching probability from the correlation factorization, obtain its reverse bound using a fixed relative core separation, and apply quasimultiplicativity. -> Sharp segment and annular alternating-arm comparisons.
- **step-disk-diagnostic-convergence** [claim](#stmt-lem-disk-diagnostic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-disk-diagnostic): Screen remote insertion cores, truncate excursions and passages, and use continuity of the resulting finite-resolution loop tests. -> Disk diagnostic limit at permissible continuity radii.
- **step-edge-to-disk-calibration** [claim](#stmt-prop-disk-calibration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-disk-calibration): Compare disk and edge signed expectations relatively and divide the microscopic amplitude by the nonzero diagnostic limit. -> Positive deterministic disk calibration factors with relative power control.
- **step-second-moment-replacement** [claim](#stmt-prop-l2-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-l2-replacement): Replace separated probe factors conditionally using calibrated reaching probabilities and common exterior verdicts, then control nearby pairs by integrable arm bounds. -> Second-moment replacement of microscopic tests by disk tests.
- **step-whole-square-content-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-whole-content) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-whole-content): Transport interior content conformally, integrate the Euclidean Green upper bound near the boundary, and remove boundary cutoffs. -> Finite whole-square content and projected neighborhood convergence with boundary-strip control.
- **step-joint-limit-identification** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-whole-content-2): Identify fixed-radius disk tests by ordered-curve convergence, combine replacement with content convergence, and use boundary estimates and tightness to identify the full measure. -> Joint ordered-curve and counting-measure convergence, including total mass and a single deterministic normalization.

The constants can depend on $p,M$ and $q$.*\n\n\n*For either choice of sign, the corresponding multiplicative statement has analytic continuation along compact paths from zero in $$\\mathcal D_p^\\pm\n =\\{\\mathbf t\\in\\mathbb R^p:\\ \\sum_jt_j=0,\n                         \\ \\pm t_j\\in I_\\lambda\\text{ for every }j\\}.$$ More precisely, on a sufficiently thin complex neighborhood of each such compact path within the neutral hyperplane $\\sum_jt_j=0$, there is a holomorphic function $M_p$ such that $$\\label{eq:coulomb-continued}\n \\frac{C(\\mathbf t;\\mathbf u)}\n {\\displaystyle\\prod_{i<j}|u_i-u_j|^{2At_it_j}}\n =M_p(\\mathbf t)+O(n^{-c}).$$ The positive constants $c$ and $\\xi$ may decrease with the path. Near zero, $M_p(\\mathbf t)=\\exp(\\sum_jf(t_j))$. The error in <a href=\"#eq:coulomb-continued\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:coulomb-continued\">[eq:coulomb-continued]</a> is additive; a relative error is asserted only where $M_p$ is nonzero.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-joint-curve-and-occupation**: The ordered interface and its normalized traversal measure converge jointly to a curve and its Minkowski-content measure belonging to the same SLE sample.
- **invariant-traversal-counting**: The discrete measure counts full medial-edge traversals. A full medial edge cannot recur, although a medial vertex can be visited twice.
- **invariant-conditioned-exterior-agreement**: The conditioned coupling makes the band and exterior agree with power accuracy, while preserving the transmitted color regions and the exterior pairing.
- **invariant-second-moment-replacement**: Microscopic occupation tests can be replaced by calibrated disk probes in second moment, with the mesh limit taken before the disk radius vanishes.
- **invariant-whole-square-content**: Interior Minkowski content extends by zero to the boundary as a finite random measure, and projected whole-plane neighborhood areas converge against continuous tests on the closed square.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-trace-implies-occupation**: Infer occupation-measure or total-step convergence directly from geometric trace convergence. -> Shape convergence leaves the microscopic normalization undetermined and does not exclude a slowly varying factor.
- **anti-use-parameter-extrapolation**: Apply the normalization beyond the stated cluster-weight interval or assume uniform estimates near its upper endpoint. -> The theorem fixes the cluster weight, and the source explicitly disclaims uniformity at the excluded endpoint.
- **anti-use-length-or-vertex-count**: Replace the traversal measure by Euclidean length or by a count of distinct medial vertices. -> The measure counts traversals, and a medial vertex can be visited twice.
- **anti-use-ordinary-arm-separation**: Treat separation of chosen arms as sufficient to erase the microscopic starting configuration from the exterior. -> Arm separation alone does not fix the exterior pairing of exposed strand ends.
- **anti-use-signed-positive-comparison**: Apply positive-law comparison directly to the remaining electric loop factor. -> The remaining loop factor can be signed, so positivity-based comparisons do not control its conditional expectation directly.
- **anti-use-interior-tests-for-total-mass**: Conclude convergence of total mass from convergence against compactly supported interior tests alone. -> Interior tests do not control mass accumulating near the boundary.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal result is joint convergence in law along an unbounded mesh sequence to a random continuum measure. Its normalization is established through analytic limits and probabilistic couplings, rather than a finite computation with a complete executable oracle. The source states: "The value of $c(q)$ depends on the stated discrete and continuum units; its numerical evaluation is not needed." Finite enumeration of lattice configurations would not certify the asserted continuum convergence or identify the normalization constant.

| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The angular-row representation concerns bounded holomorphic operators on a plane Hilbert space. It supplies no hardware mapping, arithmetic format, or accelerator construction. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Radial complex stability establishes nonvanishing and comparison of loop partition functions. It supplies no cryptographic primitive, adversarial model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The separated height covariance is a probabilistic input for identifying an electric constant. It does not construct a learning rule, a trained model, or a statistical prediction guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Coulomb formula describes asymptotic electric correlations under neutrality and geometric separation. It provides neither an optimization objective nor a procedure for finding optimal configurations. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The reference band transmits primal and dual color regions in a planar random-cluster configuration. Its exterior-pairing property does not construct a communication protocol or a distributed consistency mechanism. |
| other (Mathematical probability and critical lattice models) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs a reference annular band from actual primal and dual crossings, joins their lanes by a winding barrier, and uses the resulting strand pairing to couple conditioned lattice configurations. This is a concrete probabilistic construction supporting occupation-measure convergence, although it is not supplied as an executable sampler. |

With the angular coordinate normalized so that the strip is $|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Re}}z|<\\pi/2$, marked rows $A^D(z)$ are bounded and holomorphic, satisfy $\\|A^D(z)\\|\\le2\\|D\\|$, and obey $$A^D(z+ib)=U_bA^D(z)U_b^{-1},\\qquad U_b=e^{ibB},$$ where $B$ is self-adjoint. For a diagonal unitary twist, the normalized commuting row family has scalar spectral functions which, under $w=e^{iz}$, are inner in the right half-plane. They continue without zeros through a common-width band at each strip edge. In particular, their zeros lie in a fixed cone $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Re}}w_j\\ge c_*|w_j|$, and their general factorization has the form $$f(w)=\\eta\\exp(-a_*w-b_*/w)\\prod_j\\beta_j(w),\\qquad\n a_*,b_*\\ge0,\\quad |\\eta|=1,$$ where $\\beta_j$ are normalized half-plane Blaschke factors and $\\sum_j\\mathop{\\mathrm{Re}}w_j/(1+|w_j|^2)<\\infty$. The following bounds are uniform over the above circuit and plane states and over $v\\in V$.*\n\n\n\n*For states $\\gamma,\\gamma'$ of sizes $R,R'\\ge r_0$, $$\\label{eq:radial-comparison}\n \\left|\\log\\frac{F(\\gamma;v)}{F(\\gamma';v)}\\right|\n       \\le C\\bigl(1+|\\log(R/R')|\\bigr).$$ There is $\\delta>0$ such that every $F(\\gamma;ve^w)$ is nonzero for $|w|<\\delta$. Its logarithm, continued from its real value at $w=0$, has absolute value at most $C\\log(2+R)$. Moreover $$\\label{eq:radial-complex-ratio}\n \\left|\\log\\frac{F(\\gamma;ve^w)}{F(\\gamma;v)}\n       -\\log\\frac{F(\\gamma';ve^w)}{F(\\gamma';v)}\\right|\n \\le C|w|\\bigl(1+|\\log(R/R')|\\bigr).$$*\n\n*For two states of comparable size $R$, their positively tilted laws can be coupled so that their configurations agree inside a common surrounding primal-open circuit containing $B(u,s)$, except with probability $C(s/R)^c$, for $r_0\\le s\\le R/C$. Conditional on the common circuit, the common continuation has its circuit law tilted by $v$.*\n\n\n*Finally, let $F_{\\ge h}(\\gamma;v)$ count only the eligible loops with $R_u(\\ell)\\ge h$. For comparable starts of size $R$, $$\\label{eq:radial-truncation}\n \\frac{F_{\\ge\\epsilon R}(\\gamma;v)/\n             F_{\\ge\\epsilon R}(\\gamma';v)}\n      {F(\\gamma;v)/F(\\gamma';v)}=1+o(1)$$ uniformly as $\\epsilon\\downarrow0$ and $\\epsilon R\\to\\infty$. The same real comparison and regeneration bounds hold with any such lower cutoff on the counted radii.",
          "source_object": "stmt-prop-radial"
        }
      ],
